`README.md`:

```md
# game-hacking

Console and PC game hacking: file-format tools, script disassemblers, a
translation patch workflow and an emulator patch. Most of it was written
between 2013 and 2017 while taking a specific game apart, and each folder is a
separate small project.

No game files are in this repository. Every tool here works on files from a
copy of the game that you supply.

## Entries

| Entry | Target | What it does | Technique | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [`chdssf`](chdssf/) | SSF (Sega Saturn emulator, Windows) | Lets SSF boot a CHD disc image without a virtual drive | DLL injection, import-table hooks, a small emulated SCSI drive | Builds with 32-bit MinGW. Needs a `libchd.dll` that is not included. |
| [`clock-tower`](clock-tower/) | Clock Tower, Japanese Windows release | Disassembles the game script, dumps the text and writes it back with every scene offset corrected | Bytecode reverse engineering, text injection | Works. Python 3, round-trip tested. |
| [`ys-celceta`](ys-celceta/) | Ys: Memories of Celceta, PC | Script unpacker, disassembler and assembler, text-table compilers, texture and archive extractors | Bytecode reverse engineering, LZSS decompression | Python 2.7 as written. Not run since. |
| [`culdcept`](culdcept/) | Culdcept, Dreamcast and PS2 | Unpacks the data archives, decodes hidden cards, dumps cards, sprites and maps | Container carving, a hand-broken delta cipher, pixel-format conversion | Three scripts tested on Python 3. The rest is Python 2.7 and needs an LH5 decompressor that is not included. |
| [`castlevania`](castlevania/) | Castlevania: Lords of Shadow 1/2 and Mirror of Fate, PC | Extracts `.packed` archives; extracts and rebuilds `.pack` archives | Archive format reverse engineering | Works on Python 3 against archives built to the documented layout. Not re-run on game files. |
| [`dosbox-loader`](dosbox-loader/) | DOS and Windows 98 games under DOSBox-X | Keeps a clean OS image, one layer per installed game and a throwaway layer for saves | Differencing VHD images | Python 3. Needs DOSBox-X and your own base images. |
| [`keep-clicking-and-nobody-explodes`](keep-clicking-and-nobody-explodes/) | Keep Talking and Nobody Explodes | Browser page for the expert: tracks the bomb's details and solves the standard modules | Plain HTML and JavaScript | Works. Open `index.html`. |
| [`small-tools`](small-tools/) | Darkstone, I Wanna Be the Boshy, Project Zomboid | An archive extractor, a save-file decryptor and a quick-save helper | One file each | Two tested on Python 3. The third is Windows-only and untested here. |

Each folder has its own README with usage, what you should see and the limits.

## Write-ups

Three of these have a longer write-up on my blog:

- [Hacking Clocktower - The First Fear](https://batteryshark.github.io/2014/08/07/hacking-clocktower-the-first-fear.html)
- [Culdcept Series](https://batteryshark.github.io/2013/11/12/culdcept-series.html)
- [Hacking 'I Wanna be the Boshy' Game Saves](https://batteryshark.github.io/2014/10/03/hacking-i-wanna-be-the-boshy-game-saves.html)

## Requirements

- Python 3 for the tested tools. `castlevania/BFPKTool.py` also needs
  `pycryptodome` for encrypted archives.
- Python 2.7 for `ys-celceta` and most of `culdcept`. They also want `Pillow`
  for anything that writes an image.
- A 32-bit MinGW toolchain and CMake for `chdssf`.

## License

MIT — see [LICENSE](LICENSE). `dosbox-loader` carries its own copy. The
bundled jQuery, jQuery UI and D3 files in `keep-clicking-and-nobody-explodes`
remain under their own licences.

```

`castlevania/BFPKTool.py`:

```py
'''
	BFPKTool - A 'packed' format utility for Castlevania: Lords of Shadow 1/2
									2014 - rFx

	Usage: python3 BFPKTool.py d target.packed

	Version 3 archives have an AES-256-CBC encrypted file table. The key is
	not part of this file: put it, as 64 hex digits, in the BFPK_V3_KEY
	environment variable. Reading version 3 also needs pycryptodome.
'''

PROJECT_VERSION = 0.75
import io,os,sys,struct,zlib,binascii

def usage():
	print("Usage: %s d target.packed" % sys.argv[0])
	exit(1)

def fatal(msg):
	print(msg)
	exit(1)

BFPK_VERSION = {
	0:"RAW",
	1:"ZLIB",
	2:"MULTI_ZLIB",
	3:"ENCRYPTED"

}

def get_v3_key():
	key_hex = os.environ.get("BFPK_V3_KEY","")
	try:
		key = binascii.unhexlify(key_hex)
	except (binascii.Error,ValueError):
		key = b""
	if(len(key) != 32):
		fatal("This archive has an encrypted file table. Set BFPK_V3_KEY to the 32-byte key as 64 hex digits.")
	return key

def gen_file_table(f,num_files,version):
	curr_file = 0
	file_table = []
	while curr_file < num_files:
		#Read size of file path.

		fname_sz = struct.unpack("<I",f.read(4))[0]

		fname = f.read(fname_sz).decode("ascii").replace("\\","/")

		file_size = struct.unpack("<I",f.read(4))[0]
		file_offset = struct.unpack("<I",f.read(4))[0]
		if(version != "ENCRYPTED" and version != "ENCRYPTED_RAW"):
			deleted = struct.unpack("<I",f.read(4))[0]
		else:
			deleted = 0
		curr_file +=1
		file_table.append({"path":fname,"offset":file_offset,"size":file_size,"deleted":deleted})
	return file_table


def dump_packed_file(inpath):
	print("Dumping %s..." % inpath)
	f = open(inpath,"rb")
	magic = f.read(4)

	if(magic != b"BFPK"):
		fatal("%s is not a valid BPFK file." % inpath)

	version = struct.unpack("B",f.read(1))[0]

	if(not version in BFPK_VERSION.keys()):
		fatal("%s has an unsupported archive version." % inpath)

	version = BFPK_VERSION[version]
	version_alt = struct.unpack("B",f.read(1))[0]
	if(version == "MULTI_ZLIB" and version_alt == 0):
		version = "ENCRYPTED_RAW"
	padding = f.read(2)
	if(version == "ENCRYPTED" or version == "ENCRYPTED_RAW"):
		#Decrypt File Table
		from Crypto.Cipher import AES
		ftable_sz = struct.unpack("<I",f.read(4))[0]
		ftable_iv = f.read(16)
		file_table_bin = f.read(ftable_sz)
		aes = AES.new(get_v3_key(), AES.MODE_CBC, ftable_iv)
		file_table_bin = aes.decrypt(file_table_bin)
		num_files = struct.unpack("<I",file_table_bin[:4])[0]
		file_table = gen_file_table(io.BytesIO(file_table_bin[4:]),num_files,version)

	else:
		if(version == "MULTI_ZLIB"):
			max_page_size = struct.unpack("<I",f.read(4))[0]
		num_files = struct.unpack("<I",f.read(4))[0]
		file_table = gen_file_table(f,num_files,version)
	#Make Root Directory
	root_dir,root_ext = os.path.splitext(inpath)
	if(not os.path.exists(root_dir)):
		os.makedirs(root_dir)

	#Process File table
	for cf in file_table:
		print("%s" % cf["path"])
		# Deleted entries do not point to valid payload data.
		if(cf["deleted"] == 1):

			continue
		parent_dir,fname = os.path.split(cf["path"])
		if(parent_dir != ""):
			if(not os.path.exists(os.path.join(root_dir,parent_dir))):
				os.makedirs(os.path.join(root_dir,parent_dir))
		g = open(os.path.join(root_dir,cf["path"]),"wb")
		f.seek(cf["offset"],0)
		if(version == "RAW" or version == "ENCRYPTED_RAW"):
			g.write(f.read(cf["size"]))
		if(version == "ZLIB" or version == "ENCRYPTED"):
			comp_sz = struct.unpack("<I",f.read(4))[0]
			g.write(zlib.decompress(f.read(comp_sz)))
		if(version == "MULTI_ZLIB"):
			total_chunks_sz = struct.unpack("<I",f.read(4))[0]
			curr_offset = 0
			while(curr_offset < total_chunks_sz):
				comp_sz = struct.unpack("<I",f.read(4))[0]
				g.write(zlib.decompress(f.read(comp_sz)))
				#We can't count the compressed size value.
				curr_offset += (comp_sz + 4)


		g.close()

	f.close()

if(__name__=="__main__"):
	print("BFPKTool v %s" % PROJECT_VERSION)
	if(len(sys.argv) != 3):
		usage()
	if(not os.path.exists(sys.argv[2])):
		usage()
	opcode = sys.argv[1]
	if(opcode == "c"):
		fatal("Creating archives was never implemented.")
	if(opcode != "d"):
		usage()

	dump_packed_file(sys.argv[2])

```

`castlevania/README.md`:

```md
# Castlevania archive tools

Two small tools for the archive formats MercurySteam used in the PC releases
of Castlevania: Lords of Shadow, Lords of Shadow 2 and Mirror of Fate.

## What is inside

| File | Game | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| `BFPKTool.py` | Lords of Shadow 1 and 2 | Extracts `.packed` (BFPK) archives. |
| `packtool.py` | Mirror of Fate | Extracts `.pack` archives and builds them from a folder. |

## BFPK

A BFPK file starts with the magic `BFPK`, a version byte and a variant byte.
The file table follows: for each file a length-prefixed path, its size, its
offset and, in unencrypted tables, a deleted flag. The tool handles these
storage types:

| Version | Storage |
|---|---|
| 0 | Raw |
| 1 | Each file is one zlib stream |
| 2 | Each file is a run of zlib chunks |
| 3 | zlib, with the file table AES-256-CBC encrypted |
| 2, variant 0 | Raw, with the file table encrypted |

```sh
python3 BFPKTool.py d data.packed     # -> data/...
```

Files are written to a folder named after the archive. Entries flagged as
deleted are listed but skipped.

The key for encrypted tables is not in this repository. Supply it as 64 hex
digits in the `BFPK_V3_KEY` environment variable. Encrypted archives also need
`pycryptodome`.

## .pack

A `.pack` file is three 32-bit values (table size plus 8, data size, file
count), then one 1032-byte entry per file (a 1024-byte path, start offset, end
offset), then the data.

```sh
python3 packtool.py d data.pack       # -> data/...
python3 packtool.py c data            # -> data.pack in the current folder
```

## Status and limits

- Both were written in 2014 for Python 2 and ported to Python 3 for this
  repository.
- Checked in November 2026 on Python 3.9 with archives built by a test script
  to the layouts above: all five BFPK storage types extract correctly (the
  encrypted ones with a random test key), and a folder packed with
  `packtool.py` extracts back identical. Neither tool was re-run on real game
  archives.
- `BFPKTool.py` cannot create archives. That half was never written.
- `packtool.py` does not add any alignment padding when it builds a pack. The
  original note says the game might want some. A rebuilt pack was not tested
  in the game here.

No game files are included.

```

`castlevania/packtool.py`:

```py
'''
Mercurysteam utilty - extract/repack .pack 1.0 (Castlevania: Mirror of Fate)
            2014 rFx
'''
import os,sys,struct

'''
    Packfile notes:
    0x00 0x4 Ftable size
    0x04 0x4 data size after ftable
    0x08 0x04 number of files

    File Entry -- 1032 bytes (0x408) - basically 1024 bytes for filename and 8 bytes for beg_end
    0x00 0x?? File path cstr()
    0x400 0x4 File begin
    0x404 0x4 File end


'''

ENTRY_SZ = 1032
NAME_SZ = 1024

def usage():
    print("%s d/c target.pack " % sys.argv[0])
    exit(1)


def dump_packfile(inpath):
    file_table = []
    f = open(inpath,"rb")
    ftable_sz = struct.unpack("<I",f.read(4))[0]
    data_sz = struct.unpack("<I",f.read(4))[0]
    num_files = struct.unpack("<I",f.read(4))[0]

    #Populate File Table
    for i in range(0,num_files):
        file_entry={"path":"","offset":0,"size":0}
        file_entry["path"] = f.read(NAME_SZ).split(b"\x00")[0].decode("ascii").replace("\\","/")
        file_entry["offset"] = struct.unpack("<I",f.read(4))[0]
        file_entry["size"] = struct.unpack("<I",f.read(4))[0] - file_entry["offset"]
        file_table.append(file_entry)

    #Make Root Directory
    root_dir,root_ext = os.path.splitext(inpath)
    if(not os.path.exists(root_dir)):
        os.makedirs(root_dir)

    #Process File table
    for cf in file_table:
        parent_dir,fname = os.path.split(cf["path"])
        if(parent_dir != ""):
            if(not os.path.exists(os.path.join(root_dir,parent_dir))):
                os.makedirs(os.path.join(root_dir,parent_dir))
        g = open(os.path.join(root_dir,cf["path"]),"wb")
        f.seek(cf["offset"],0)
        g.write(f.read(cf["size"]))
        g.close()

    f.close()

def create_packfile(outpath):
    outpath = os.path.normpath(outpath)
    file_table = []
    #Running total of data size
    data_sz = 0
    for dirpath, dnames, fnames in os.walk(outpath):
        dnames.sort()
        for f in sorted(fnames):
            file_entry={"path":"","size":0}
            file_entry["path"] = os.path.join(dirpath, f)
            file_entry["size"] = os.path.getsize(file_entry["path"])
            data_sz += file_entry["size"]
            file_table.append(file_entry)
    #Create new .pack file.
    base,dirname = os.path.split(outpath)
    g = open(dirname+".pack","wb")
    # Write size of file table + 8
    g.write(struct.pack("<I",(len(file_table) * ENTRY_SZ)+8))
    # Write size of data
    g.write(struct.pack("<I",data_sz))
    #Write number of files
    g.write(struct.pack("<I",len(file_table)))

    #Virtual offset for constructing the table
    #This is where the files will eventually be written.
    voffset = (len(file_table) * ENTRY_SZ) + 12

    #Add each file to the pack.	We mIGHT need 64-68 bytes of padding, but I'm not sure.
    for cf in file_table:
        # Paths in the table are relative to the packed folder.
        name = os.path.relpath(cf["path"],outpath).replace(os.sep,"/").encode("ascii")
        if(len(name) >= NAME_SZ):
            print("Path too long for a pack entry: %s" % cf["path"])
            exit(1)
        f_entry = name + bytes(NAME_SZ - len(name))
        f_entry += struct.pack("<I",voffset)
        voffset += cf["size"]
        f_entry += struct.pack("<I",voffset)
        #Write File table to file.
        g.write(f_entry)

    for cf in file_table:
        f = open(cf["path"],"rb")
        g.write(f.read())
        f.close()
    g.close()
    print("Done!")

if(__name__=="__main__"):
    print("Mercury Steam .PACK Utility")
    if(len(sys.argv) < 3):
        usage()
    if(sys.argv[1] != "d" and sys.argv[1] != "c"):
        usage()
    if(sys.argv[1] == "d"):
        if(not os.path.exists(sys.argv[2])):
            usage()
        dump_packfile(sys.argv[2])
    if(sys.argv[1] == "c"):
        create_packfile(sys.argv[2])

```

`chdssf/ARCHITECTURE.md`:

```md
# Architecture

## System Map

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    User["User runs SSFLoader.exe game.chd"]
    Loader["src/loader\ncreates SSF.exe suspended"]
    Inject["remote LoadLibraryA(\"ssf_patch.dll\") shellcode"]
    Patch["src/ssf_patch\nDllMain installs hooks"]
    Win32["SSF Win32 API calls\nCreateFileA / DeviceIoControl / GetDriveTypeA"]
    SPTD["virtual SPTD/SCSI responder"]
    Libchd["libchd.dll"]
    CHD["Saturn CHD image"]

    User --> Loader --> Inject --> Patch
    Patch --> Win32 --> SPTD --> Libchd --> CHD
```

## Components

`src/loader/main.c` is the process launcher. It resolves `SSF.exe` and
`ssf_patch.dll` relative to the current directory, starts SSF suspended, writes
the DLL path and a small `LoadLibraryA` shellcode stub into the SSF process, and
then resumes the SSF main thread.

`src/ssf_patch/main.c` is the injected DLL entrypoint. On process attach it
initializes CHD/SPTD state and installs filesystem/device hooks.

`src/ssf_patch/global.c` contains memory patch and import-address-table hook
helpers. The active hooks replace SSF's imported `KERNEL32.dll` functions by
name.

`src/ssf_patch/fs.c` is the Win32 API shim. It detects attempts to open optical
device paths, returns a sentinel handle, and routes SCSI pass-through
`DeviceIoControl` calls into the virtual SPTD layer.

`src/ssf_patch/sptd.c` translates the subset of SCSI commands SSF needs into
TOC and sector reads from `libchd.dll`.

`src/ssf_patch/chd_helper.h` is a local copy of the CHD/CD-ROM structures needed
to interpret `libchd.dll` results.

## Runtime Flow

1. The user runs `SSFLoader.exe path\to\disc.chd`.
2. The loader starts `SSF.exe` in a suspended state.
3. The loader writes the absolute path to `ssf_patch.dll` into SSF memory.
4. The loader writes shellcode that calls `LoadLibraryA` with that DLL path.
5. The loader changes the suspended thread's `Eip` to the shellcode and resumes
   the thread.
6. `ssf_patch.dll` loads inside SSF, reads the CHD path from SSF's command line,
   and opens it through `libchd.dll`.
7. The patch replaces selected SSF imports from `KERNEL32.dll`.
8. When SSF opens or queries an optical drive, the patch returns a virtual
   handle and answers SCSI pass-through commands from CHD data.

## Load-Bearing Weirdness

- The command line passed to `SSF.exe` still names `SSFLoader.exe`. This lets
  the injected DLL read the CHD path as `argv[1]` inside SSF.
- The loader waits before freeing remote memory because the injected shellcode
  runs after the target thread resumes. The wait is crude, but removing it
  breaks the injection.
- `CHDDriveVirtual` is the virtual INQUIRY vendor/device string SSF sees.
- Audio sectors may be byte-swapped on read. The original note says Archive.org
  Saturn CHD audio was endian-reversed for the tested images.
- The SCSI command set is intentionally tiny: only the commands SSF appeared to
  need were implemented.

## External Dependencies

- `SSF.exe`: not included.
- `libchd.dll`: not included, and its source is not in this repository.
- Windows `KERNEL32.dll` and `SHELL32.dll`.

## Known Risks

- Runtime behavior is tied to 32-bit Windows process internals.
- The project has no automated tests. Meaningful validation requires SSF and a
  known-good Saturn CHD.
- The `libchd.dll` ABI was recovered from usage and export names, not headers.
- The patch assumes SSF imports the hooked APIs by name from `KERNEL32.dll`.
- Direct IAT patching and remote-thread context editing can be blocked by
  security software.

```

`chdssf/CMakeLists.txt`:

```txt
cmake_minimum_required(VERSION 3.20)
project(CHDSSF LANGUAGES C)

set(CMAKE_C_STANDARD 11)
set(CMAKE_C_STANDARD_REQUIRED ON)
set(CMAKE_C_EXTENSIONS OFF)

if(NOT WIN32)
    message(FATAL_ERROR "CHDSSF is a Win32 process patch for SSF and only builds for Windows targets.")
endif()

function(chdssf_configure_win32_target target_name)
    target_compile_features(${target_name} PRIVATE c_std_11)

    if(MINGW)
        target_compile_options(${target_name} PRIVATE -m32)
        target_link_options(${target_name} PRIVATE -m32)
    endif()

    if(NOT CMAKE_SIZEOF_VOID_P EQUAL 4)
        message(WARNING
            "${target_name} is expected to be built with a 32-bit Windows toolchain. "
            "Use the MSYS2 mingw32 compiler or a Win32 generator.")
    endif()
endfunction()

add_executable(SSFLoader src/loader/main.c)
chdssf_configure_win32_target(SSFLoader)

add_subdirectory(src/ssf_patch)

```

`chdssf/README.md`:

```md
# CHDSSF

A Win32 patch that lets the SSF Sega Saturn emulator boot a CHD disc image
directly.

## Why this exists

When this was written, SSF read games from an optical drive. To run a disc
image you mounted it in a virtual drive first, and it could not read CHD, the
compressed format most Saturn sets are stored in. CHDSSF sits between SSF and
Windows and answers SSF's drive commands from the CHD file, so nothing has to
be mounted or converted.

## How it works

- `SSFLoader.exe` starts `SSF.exe` suspended, writes a short `LoadLibraryA`
  stub into it and points the main thread at the stub, which loads
  `ssf_patch.dll`.
- `ssf_patch.dll` replaces four of SSF's `KERNEL32.dll` imports: `CreateFileA`,
  `DeviceIoControl`, `GetDriveTypeA` and `CloseHandle`.
- When SSF opens a device path it gets a fake handle. SCSI pass-through
  requests on that handle are answered by a small emulated drive that reports
  itself as `CHDDriveVirtual`.
- The emulated drive handles the six commands SSF sends: TEST UNIT READY,
  REQUEST SENSE, INQUIRY, READ TOC, PREVENT/ALLOW MEDIUM REMOVAL and READ CD.
  Table-of-contents and sector data come from the CHD through `libchd.dll`.

[ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md) has the full flow and the assumptions that
matter if you change it.

## Layout

```text
src/loader/      SSFLoader.exe: starts SSF and injects the patch
src/ssf_patch/   ssf_patch.dll: import hooks and the emulated SCSI drive
ARCHITECTURE.md  runtime flow, assumptions and risks
```

## Build

The target is 32-bit Windows. With MSYS2 MinGW32 on Windows:

```powershell
cmake -S . -B build -G "MinGW Makefiles"
cmake --build build
```

Cross-compiling from macOS or Linux with mingw-w64 also works:

```sh
cmake -S . -B build -G Ninja -DCMAKE_SYSTEM_NAME=Windows \
      -DCMAKE_C_COMPILER=i686-w64-mingw32-gcc
cmake --build build
```

Output: `build/SSFLoader.exe` and `build/src/ssf_patch/ssf_patch.dll`, both
PE32.

## Run

1. Put `SSFLoader.exe`, `ssf_patch.dll` and `libchd.dll` next to `SSF.exe`.
2. Start it with a disc image:

   ```powershell
   .\SSFLoader.exe .\game.chd
   ```

3. In SSF the drive shows up as `CHDDriveVirtual`. If SSF starts without the
   disc, select that drive and choose `CD Close` from the Hardware menu.

The patch logs each command with `OutputDebugString`, so DebugView shows what
SSF asked for if a disc does not read.

## Status and limits

- Written in 2017 against the SSF build current then. It has not been tried
  on later builds.
- 32-bit only. The loader edits the thread's `Eip` and the stub assumes 4-byte
  addresses.
- `libchd.dll` is not in this repository. The patch expects a DLL exporting
  `libchd_cdrom_open`, `libchd_cdrom_get_toc` and `libchd_cdrom_read_data`,
  returning the track structures MAME's CHD CD-ROM code uses. Without that DLL
  the patch cannot read a disc.
- Audio sectors are byte-swapped on read, because the CHD sets it was tested
  with stored audio in the other byte order.
- Only the commands SSF was seen to send are implemented.
- The current source builds cleanly with mingw-w64 (checked November 2026).
  It has not been run against SSF since the original work.
- Injection and import patching look like malware to some security software.

## Credits

- SSF is by Shima. It is not included.
- The track and table-of-contents structures in `src/ssf_patch/chd_helper.h`
  follow MAME's `cdrom.h`.

```

`chdssf/src/loader/main.c`:

```c
/*
 * Loader stub for injecting the virtual SPTD patch into SSF.
 */
#define WIN32_LEAN_AND_MEAN

#include <windows.h>

#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <string.h>

#define PATH_BUFFER_SIZE 4096
#define LOAD_LIBRARY_SHELLCODE_SIZE 22
#define REMOTE_PATCH_WAIT_MS 8000

static char inject_dll_path[PATH_BUFFER_SIZE];
static char full_exe_path[PATH_BUFFER_SIZE];

static void print_last_error(const char *action)
{
    fprintf(stderr, "%s failed with Win32 error %lu\n", action, GetLastError());
}

static BOOL create_load_library_shellcode(
    DWORD return_address,
    DWORD dll_path_address,
    unsigned char **shellcode,
    int *shellcode_size)
{
    HMODULE kernel32 = GetModuleHandleA("kernel32.dll");
    FARPROC load_library = kernel32 == NULL ? NULL : GetProcAddress(kernel32, "LoadLibraryA");

    if (load_library == NULL) {
        return FALSE;
    }

    unsigned char *return_bytes = (unsigned char *) &return_address;
    unsigned char *path_bytes = (unsigned char *) &dll_path_address;
    DWORD load_library_address = (DWORD) (ULONG_PTR) load_library;
    unsigned char *api_bytes = (unsigned char *) &load_library_address;

    unsigned char stub[] = {
        0x68, return_bytes[0], return_bytes[1], return_bytes[2], return_bytes[3],
        0x9C,
        0x60,
        0x68, path_bytes[0], path_bytes[1], path_bytes[2], path_bytes[3],
        0xB8, api_bytes[0], api_bytes[1], api_bytes[2], api_bytes[3],
        0xFF, 0xD0,
        0x61,
        0x9D,
        0xC3
    };

    *shellcode_size = LOAD_LIBRARY_SHELLCODE_SIZE;
    *shellcode = malloc(LOAD_LIBRARY_SHELLCODE_SIZE);
    if (*shellcode == NULL) {
        return FALSE;
    }

    memcpy(*shellcode, stub, LOAD_LIBRARY_SHELLCODE_SIZE);
    return TRUE;
}

int main(int argc, char *argv[])
{
    (void) argv;

    if (argc < 2) {
        fprintf(stderr, "Usage: SSFLoader.exe <path-to-disc.chd>\n");
        return 2;
    }

    if (GetFullPathNameA("ssf_patch.dll", PATH_BUFFER_SIZE, inject_dll_path, NULL) == 0) {
        print_last_error("Resolving ssf_patch.dll");
        return 1;
    }

    if (GetFullPathNameA("SSF.exe", PATH_BUFFER_SIZE, full_exe_path, NULL) == 0) {
        print_last_error("Resolving SSF.exe");
        return 1;
    }

    PROCESS_INFORMATION process_info;
    STARTUPINFOA startup_info;
    LPSTR command_line = GetCommandLineA();

    ZeroMemory(&startup_info, sizeof(startup_info));
    ZeroMemory(&process_info, sizeof(process_info));
    startup_info.cb = sizeof(startup_info);

    if (!CreateProcessA(
            full_exe_path,
            command_line,
            NULL,
            NULL,
            FALSE,
            CREATE_SUSPENDED,
            NULL,
            NULL,
            &startup_info,
            &process_info)) {
        print_last_error("Starting SSF.exe");
        return 1;
    }

    size_t dll_path_size = strlen(inject_dll_path) + 1;
    LPVOID remote_dll_path = VirtualAllocEx(
        process_info.hProcess,
        NULL,
        dll_path_size,
        MEM_COMMIT,
        PAGE_READWRITE);
    if (remote_dll_path == NULL) {
        print_last_error("Allocating remote DLL path");
        return 1;
    }

    CONTEXT context;
    ZeroMemory(&context, sizeof(context));
    context.ContextFlags = CONTEXT_CONTROL;

    if (!GetThreadContext(process_info.hThread, &context)) {
        print_last_error("Reading SSF thread context");
        return 1;
    }

    unsigned char *shellcode = NULL;
    int shellcode_size = 0;
    if (!create_load_library_shellcode(
            context.Eip,
            (DWORD) (ULONG_PTR) remote_dll_path,
            &shellcode,
            &shellcode_size)) {
        print_last_error("Creating LoadLibrary shellcode");
        return 1;
    }

    LPVOID remote_shellcode = VirtualAllocEx(
        process_info.hProcess,
        NULL,
        shellcode_size,
        MEM_COMMIT,
        PAGE_EXECUTE_READWRITE);
    if (remote_shellcode == NULL) {
        print_last_error("Allocating remote shellcode");
        free(shellcode);
        return 1;
    }

    if (!WriteProcessMemory(process_info.hProcess, remote_dll_path, inject_dll_path, dll_path_size, NULL)) {
        print_last_error("Writing DLL path into SSF");
        free(shellcode);
        return 1;
    }

    if (!WriteProcessMemory(process_info.hProcess, remote_shellcode, shellcode, shellcode_size, NULL)) {
        print_last_error("Writing shellcode into SSF");
        free(shellcode);
        return 1;
    }

    context.Eip = (DWORD) (ULONG_PTR) remote_shellcode;
    context.ContextFlags = CONTEXT_CONTROL;
    if (!SetThreadContext(process_info.hThread, &context)) {
        print_last_error("Redirecting SSF thread context");
        free(shellcode);
        return 1;
    }

    if (ResumeThread(process_info.hThread) == (DWORD) -1) {
        print_last_error("Resuming SSF");
        free(shellcode);
        return 1;
    }

    Sleep(REMOTE_PATCH_WAIT_MS);

    VirtualFreeEx(process_info.hProcess, remote_dll_path, dll_path_size, MEM_DECOMMIT);
    VirtualFreeEx(process_info.hProcess, remote_shellcode, shellcode_size, MEM_DECOMMIT);
    CloseHandle(process_info.hThread);
    CloseHandle(process_info.hProcess);
    free(shellcode);

    return 0;
}

```

`chdssf/src/ssf_patch/chd_helper.h`:

```h
#ifndef __CHD_HELPER
#define __CHD_HELPER

#define CD_MAX_TRACKS		(99)	/* AFAIK the theoretical limit */
#define CD_MAX_SECTOR_DATA	(2352)
#define CD_MAX_SUBCODE_DATA	(96)

#define CD_FRAME_SIZE		(CD_MAX_SECTOR_DATA + CD_MAX_SUBCODE_DATA)
#define CD_FRAMES_PER_HUNK	(4)

#define CD_METADATA_WORDS	(1+(CD_MAX_TRACKS * 6))
enum
{
	CD_TRACK_MODE1 = 0, 	/* mode 1 2048 bytes/sector */
	CD_TRACK_MODE1_RAW,	/* mode 1 2352 bytes/sector */
	CD_TRACK_MODE2,		/* mode 2 2336 bytes/sector */
	CD_TRACK_MODE2_FORM1,	/* mode 2 2048 bytes/sector */
	CD_TRACK_MODE2_FORM2,	/* mode 2 2324 bytes/sector */
	CD_TRACK_MODE2_FORM_MIX, /* mode 2 2336 bytes/sector */
	CD_TRACK_MODE2_RAW,	/* mode 2 2352 bytes / sector */
	CD_TRACK_AUDIO		/* redbook audio track 2352 bytes/sector (588 samples) */
};

enum
{
	CD_SUB_NORMAL = 0, 	/* "cooked" 96 bytes per sector */
	CD_SUB_RAW,		/* raw uninterleaved 96 bytes per sector */
	CD_SUB_NONE		/* no subcode data stored */
};



/***************************************************************************
    TYPE DEFINITIONS
***************************************************************************/

typedef struct _cdrom_file cdrom_file;


typedef struct _cdrom_track_info cdrom_track_info;
struct _cdrom_track_info
{
	/* fields used by CHDMAN and in MAME */
	unsigned int trktype;     /* track type */
	unsigned int subtype;     /* subcode data type */
	unsigned int datasize;    /* size of data in each sector of this track */
	unsigned int subsize;     /* size of subchannel data in each sector of this track */
	unsigned int frames;      /* number of frames in this track */
	unsigned int extraframes; /* number of "spillage" frames in this track */
	unsigned int pregap;      /* number of pregap frames */
	unsigned int postgap;     /* number of postgap frames */
	unsigned int pgtype;      /* type of sectors in pregap */
	unsigned int pgsub;       /* type of subchannel data in pregap */
	unsigned int pgdatasize;  /* size of data in each sector of the pregap */
	unsigned int pgsubsize;   /* size of subchannel data in each sector of the pregap */

	/* fields used in CHDMAN only */
	unsigned int padframes;   /* number of frames of padding to add to the end of the track; needed for GDI */

	/* fields used in MAME/MESS only */
	unsigned int logframeofs; /* logical frame of actual track data - offset by pregap size if pregap not physically present */
	unsigned int physframeofs; /* physical frame of actual track data in CHD data */
	unsigned int chdframeofs; /* frame number this track starts at on the CHD */
};


typedef struct _cdrom_track_input_info cdrom_track_input_info;
struct _cdrom_track_input_info	/* used only at compression time */
{
	char fname[CD_MAX_TRACKS][256];	/* filename for each track */
	UINT32 offset[CD_MAX_TRACKS];	/* offset in the data file for each track */
};


typedef struct _cdrom_toc cdrom_toc;
struct _cdrom_toc
{
	UINT32 numtrks;		/* number of tracks */
	cdrom_track_info tracks[CD_MAX_TRACKS];
};

static struct _cdrom_file
{
	int *			chd;				/* CHD file */
	cdrom_toc			cdtoc;				/* TOC for the CD */
	unsigned int				hunksectors;		/* sectors per hunk */
	unsigned int				cachehunk;			/* which hunk is cached */
	unsigned char *				cache;				/* cache of the current hunk */
}cdrf;

#endif
```

`chdssf/src/ssf_patch/fs.c`:

```c
// Filesystem Related Operations.

#include "global.h"
#include "sptd.h"
#include "fs.h"
// Hardcoded FS Patch Offset Constants
#define DEVICE_PATH "\\\\.\\"

#define SCSI_PASSTHROUGH_DIRECT 0x4D014
#define SCSI_HANDLE ((HANDLE)(UINT_PTR)0xB00BD001)

static int is_init_removable = 0;

// CreateFileA Hook
HANDLE __stdcall rfx_CreateFileA(LPCSTR lpFileName, DWORD dwDesiredAccess, DWORD dwShareMode, LPSECURITY_ATTRIBUTES lpSecurityAttributes, DWORD dwCreationDisposition, DWORD dwFlagsAndAttributes, HANDLE hTemplateFile){
    OutputDebugStringA("CreateFileA");
    OutputDebugStringA(lpFileName);

    if(strstr(lpFileName,DEVICE_PATH)!= 0){
        OutputDebugStringA("Optical Device Open Called.");
        return SCSI_HANDLE;
    }
    return CreateFileA(lpFileName,dwDesiredAccess,dwShareMode,lpSecurityAttributes,dwCreationDisposition,dwFlagsAndAttributes,hTemplateFile);
}

BOOL __stdcall rfx_CloseHandle(HANDLE hObject){
    if(hObject == SCSI_HANDLE){
        OutputDebugStringA("Closing Virtual SPTD Handle");
        return TRUE;
    }
    return CloseHandle(hObject);
}

BOOL __stdcall rfx_DeviceIoControl(HANDLE hDevice, DWORD dwIoControlCode, LPVOID lpInBuffer, DWORD nInBufferSize, LPVOID lpOutBuffer, DWORD nOutBufferSize, LPDWORD lpBytesReturned, LPOVERLAPPED lpOverlapped){
    if(hDevice == SCSI_HANDLE && dwIoControlCode == SCSI_PASSTHROUGH_DIRECT){
        return sptd_ioctl(hDevice,dwIoControlCode,lpInBuffer,nInBufferSize,lpOutBuffer,nOutBufferSize,lpBytesReturned,lpOverlapped);
    }
    return DeviceIoControl(hDevice,dwIoControlCode,lpInBuffer,nInBufferSize,lpOutBuffer,nOutBufferSize,lpBytesReturned,lpOverlapped);
}

// Return removable once, and that's it.
unsigned int __stdcall rfx_GetDriveTypeA(LPCSTR lpRootPathName){
    (void) lpRootPathName;

    if(is_init_removable == 0){
        is_init_removable = 1;
        return 5;
    }

    return 3;
}

// Startup Routine
void patch_fs(){
    // Save root patch
    // Various file hooks
    Hook_IAT_Name("KERNEL32.dll","CreateFileA",(uintptr_t)&rfx_CreateFileA);
    Hook_IAT_Name("KERNEL32.dll","DeviceIoControl",(uintptr_t)&rfx_DeviceIoControl);
    Hook_IAT_Name("KERNEL32.dll","GetDriveTypeA",(uintptr_t)&rfx_GetDriveTypeA);
    Hook_IAT_Name("KERNEL32.dll","CloseHandle",(uintptr_t)&rfx_CloseHandle);
}

```

`chdssf/src/ssf_patch/global.c`:

```c
#include "global.h"

// Generic replacement functions to return a specified value.
int patch_return_zero(){return 0;}
int patch_return_one(){return 1;}

// [--Memory Patch Functions--]

// Patch sz number of bytes as NOP.
void patch_memory_nop(void* addr,unsigned int sz){
    DWORD OldProtect = 0;
    VirtualProtect(addr, sz, PAGE_EXECUTE_READWRITE, &OldProtect);
    memset(addr,0x90,sz);
}

// Patch memory with an arbitrary buffer.
void patch_memory_buffer(void* addr, unsigned char* buffer, unsigned int sz){
    DWORD OldProtect = 0;
    VirtualProtect(addr, sz, PAGE_EXECUTE_READWRITE, &OldProtect);
    memcpy(addr,buffer,sz);
}

// Patch memory with an arbitrary string.
void patch_memory_str(void* addr, char* str){
    DWORD OldProtect = 0;
    unsigned int sz = strlen(str)+1;
    VirtualProtect(addr, sz, PAGE_EXECUTE_READWRITE, &OldProtect);
    strcpy(addr,str);
}

// Patch memory to add a jump to an arbitrary address.
void detour_jmp(void* new_addr,void* addr){
    DWORD OldProtect = 0;
    int jmp_distance = (int) ((uintptr_t) new_addr - ((uintptr_t) addr + 5));
    VirtualProtect((void*)addr,4096, PAGE_EXECUTE_READWRITE,&OldProtect);
    *((unsigned char*)(addr)) = 0xE9;
    *((int*)((unsigned char*)addr + 1)) = jmp_distance;
}

// Patch memory to add a call to an arbitrary address.
void detour_call(void* new_addr,void* addr){
    DWORD OldProtect = 0;
    int jmp_distance = (int) ((uintptr_t) new_addr - ((uintptr_t) addr + 5));
    VirtualProtect((void*)addr,4096, PAGE_EXECUTE_READWRITE,&OldProtect);
    *((unsigned char*)(addr)) = 0xE8;
    *((int*)((unsigned char*)addr + 1)) = jmp_distance;
}

// Patch memory to in-line replace a call to an IAT function without replacing the whole function.
void detour_function_ds(void * new_adr, void* addr){
    DWORD OldProtect = 0;
    int call = (int)(uintptr_t)(new_adr);
    VirtualProtect(addr,4096, PAGE_EXECUTE_READWRITE, &OldProtect);
    *((unsigned char*)(addr)) = 0xFF;
    *((unsigned char*)addr + 1) = 0x15;
    *((int*)((unsigned char*)addr + 2)) = call;
}

// Patch an IAT entry to replace any calls to it with a new function by ordinal.
BOOL Hook_IAT_Ordinal(char* dll_name,char*func_name,unsigned int ordinal,uintptr_t replacement_function_ptr){
    IMAGE_DOS_HEADER *pDOSHeader;
    IMAGE_NT_HEADERS *pNTHeader;
    IMAGE_IMPORT_DESCRIPTOR *ImportDirectory;
    DWORD *OriginalFirstThunk;
    DWORD *FirstThunk;
    DWORD *address;
    DWORD *func_address;
    char *module_name="";
    DWORD overwrite;
    char *name;
    ULONG_PTR Cur_Ordinal=0;
    HANDLE hHandle;
    DWORD oldProtect;
    DWORD PEHeaderOffset;
    int i=0;

    hHandle = GetModuleHandle(NULL);

    if(hHandle == NULL){
        OutputDebugStringA("there was an error in retrieving the handle");
        exit(0);
    }

    pDOSHeader = (IMAGE_DOS_HEADER *) hHandle;

    PEHeaderOffset = (DWORD) pDOSHeader->e_lfanew;

    pNTHeader = (IMAGE_NT_HEADERS *) ((DWORD) hHandle + PEHeaderOffset);

    ImportDirectory = (IMAGE_IMPORT_DESCRIPTOR *) ((DWORD) pNTHeader->OptionalHeader.DataDirectory[1].VirtualAddress + (DWORD) hHandle);
    module_name = (char *)(ImportDirectory->Name + (DWORD) hHandle);

    while(strcmp(module_name, dll_name) != 0){
        ImportDirectory++;
        module_name = (char *)(ImportDirectory->Name + (DWORD) hHandle);
    }


    OriginalFirstThunk = (DWORD *)((DWORD) ImportDirectory->OriginalFirstThunk + (DWORD) hHandle);
    FirstThunk = (DWORD *)((DWORD) ImportDirectory->FirstThunk + (DWORD) hHandle);

    while(*(OriginalFirstThunk+i) != 0x00000000){
        Cur_Ordinal = *(OriginalFirstThunk+i);
        Cur_Ordinal &= ~0x80000000;
        if(Cur_Ordinal == ordinal) {
            address=OriginalFirstThunk+i;
            break;
        }
        i++;
    }


    func_address = FirstThunk - OriginalFirstThunk + address;

    VirtualProtect(func_address, 0x4, 0x40, &oldProtect);
    overwrite = (DWORD) replacement_function_ptr;
    WriteProcessMemory(GetCurrentProcess(), func_address, &overwrite, 0x4, NULL);
    VirtualProtect(func_address, 0x4, 0x20, &oldProtect);
    return 0;

}

// Patch an IAT entry to replace any calls to it with a new function by name.
BOOL Hook_IAT_Name (char* dll_name, char* func_name,uintptr_t replacement_function_ptr){
    IMAGE_DOS_HEADER *pDOSHeader;
    IMAGE_NT_HEADERS *pNTHeader;
    IMAGE_IMPORT_DESCRIPTOR *ImportDirectory;
    DWORD *OriginalFirstThunk;
    DWORD *FirstThunk;
    DWORD *address;
    DWORD *func_address;
    char *module_name="";
    DWORD overwrite;
    char *name;
    HANDLE hHandle;
    DWORD oldProtect;
    DWORD PEHeaderOffset;
    int i=0;

    hHandle = GetModuleHandle(NULL);

    if(hHandle == NULL){
        OutputDebugStringA("there was an error in retrieving the handle");
        exit(0);
    }

    pDOSHeader = (IMAGE_DOS_HEADER *) hHandle;

    PEHeaderOffset = (DWORD) pDOSHeader->e_lfanew;

    pNTHeader = (IMAGE_NT_HEADERS *) ((DWORD) hHandle + PEHeaderOffset);

    ImportDirectory = (IMAGE_IMPORT_DESCRIPTOR *) ((DWORD) pNTHeader->OptionalHeader.DataDirectory[1].VirtualAddress + (DWORD) hHandle);
    module_name = (char *)(ImportDirectory->Name + (DWORD) hHandle);

    while(strcmp(module_name, dll_name) != 0){
        ImportDirectory++;
        module_name = (char *)(ImportDirectory->Name + (DWORD) hHandle);
    }


    OriginalFirstThunk = (DWORD *)((DWORD) ImportDirectory->OriginalFirstThunk + (DWORD) hHandle);
    FirstThunk = (DWORD *)((DWORD) ImportDirectory->FirstThunk + (DWORD) hHandle);


    while(*(OriginalFirstThunk+i) != 0x00000000){
        name = (char *) (*(OriginalFirstThunk+i) + (DWORD) hHandle + 0x2);
        if(strcmp(name, func_name) == 0)
        {
            address=OriginalFirstThunk+i;
            break;
        }
        i++;
    }

    func_address = FirstThunk - OriginalFirstThunk + address;
    VirtualProtect(func_address, 0x4, 0x40, &oldProtect);
    overwrite = (DWORD) replacement_function_ptr;
    WriteProcessMemory(GetCurrentProcess(), func_address, &overwrite, 0x4, NULL);
    VirtualProtect(func_address, 0x4, 0x20, &oldProtect);
    return 0;
}

```

`chdssf/src/ssf_patch/global.h`:

```h
#ifndef CHDSSF_GLOBAL_H
#define CHDSSF_GLOBAL_H

#include <windows.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdbool.h>
#include <stdint.h>
#include <string.h>

// Hardcoded Serial Offset Constants
#define COM1_GUNIO_HANDLE 0xB00BC001
#define COM2_JVS_HANDLE 0xB00BC003

int init_global(void);
int patch_return_zero(void);
int patch_return_one(void);
void patch_memory_nop(void* addr,unsigned int sz);
void patch_memory_buffer(void* addr, unsigned char* buffer, unsigned int sz);
void detour_jmp(void* new_addr,void* addr);
void detour_call(void* new_addr,void* addr);
void detour_function_ds(void * new_adr, void* addr);
void patch_memory_str(void* addr,char* str);
BOOL Hook_IAT_Name(char* dll_name, char* func_name,uintptr_t replacement_function_ptr);
BOOL Hook_IAT_Ordinal(char* dll_name,char*func_name,unsigned int ordinal,uintptr_t replacement_function_ptr);

#endif

`chdssf/src/ssf_patch/main.c`:

```c
#include "main.h"
#include "global.h"
#include "fs.h"
#include "sptd.h"

#include <stdio.h>
#include <Windows.h>


void patch_binary(){

    init_sptd();
    patch_fs();
}
BOOL WINAPI DllMain(HINSTANCE hinstDLL, DWORD fdwReason, LPVOID lpvReserved){
    (void) hinstDLL;
    (void) lpvReserved;

    if (fdwReason == DLL_PROCESS_ATTACH ){ patch_binary(); }
    return TRUE;
}

```

`chdssf/src/ssf_patch/sptd.c`:

```c
// Virtual SPTD layer: answers the SCSI pass-through commands SSF sends to an
// optical drive, using TOC and sector data read from a CHD through libchd.dll.

#include "sptd.h"
#include "global.h"
#include "chd_helper.h"

#include <shellapi.h>

typedef intptr_t (*libchd_cdrom_open_fn)(LPCWSTR);
typedef int (*libchd_cdrom_get_toc_fn)(struct _cdrom_file *, unsigned int *);
typedef int (*libchd_cdrom_read_data_fn)(struct _cdrom_file *, unsigned int, unsigned char *, unsigned int, unsigned char);

static HMODULE libchd_module = NULL;
static libchd_cdrom_open_fn libchd_cdrom_open = NULL;
static libchd_cdrom_get_toc_fn libchd_cdrom_get_toc = NULL;
static libchd_cdrom_read_data_fn libchd_cdrom_read_data = NULL;
static unsigned char* disc_toc = NULL;
static unsigned int disc_toc_size = 0;

unsigned int bswap32(unsigned int x)
{
    return	((x << 24) & 0xff000000 ) |
              ((x <<  8) & 0x00ff0000 ) |
              ((x >>  8) & 0x0000ff00 ) |
              ((x >> 24) & 0x000000ff );
}

unsigned short bswap16(unsigned short x)
{
    /*LINTED*/
    return ((x << 8) & 0xff00) | ((x >> 8) & 0x00ff);
}


void get_toc_data(){
    size_t toc_buffer_size = 12 + (cdrf.cdtoc.numtrks * 8);

    free(disc_toc);
    disc_toc = (unsigned char*)malloc(toc_buffer_size);
    if(disc_toc == NULL){
        disc_toc_size = 0;
        OutputDebugStringA("Unable to allocate TOC buffer");
        return;
    }

    memset(disc_toc,0x00,toc_buffer_size);

    unsigned short toc_sz = 10+(cdrf.cdtoc.numtrks*8);
    toc_sz = bswap16(toc_sz);

    memcpy(disc_toc,&toc_sz,2);
    memset(disc_toc+2,0x01,1);
    memset(disc_toc+3,cdrf.cdtoc.numtrks&0xFF,1);

    unsigned int toc_buffer_track_offset = 4;
    unsigned int leadout_start = 0;

    for(int i=0;i< cdrf.cdtoc.numtrks;i++){
        unsigned char track_info[8] = {0x00};
        if(cdrf.cdtoc.tracks[i].subtype == 0x01){
            track_info[1] = 0x14;
        }else{
            track_info[1] = 0x10;
        }

        track_info[2] = i+1;
        unsigned int tis = bswap32(cdrf.cdtoc.tracks[i].physframeofs);
        memcpy(track_info+4,&tis,4);
        memcpy(disc_toc+toc_buffer_track_offset,track_info,8);
        toc_buffer_track_offset+=8;
        leadout_start = cdrf.cdtoc.tracks[i].physframeofs + cdrf.cdtoc.tracks[i].extraframes;

    }
    unsigned char leadout_track[8] = {0x00, 0x10, 0xAA, 0x00,0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00};
    unsigned int tis = bswap32(leadout_start);
    memcpy(leadout_track+4,&tis,4);
    memset(disc_toc+3,cdrf.cdtoc.numtrks&0xFF,1);
    memcpy(disc_toc+toc_buffer_track_offset,leadout_track,8);
    disc_toc_size = (unsigned int) toc_buffer_size;

}

BOOL load_chd_file(LPCWSTR chd_path){
    if(libchd_cdrom_open == NULL){
        OutputDebugStringA("libchd_cdrom_open was not loaded");
        return FALSE;
    }

    intptr_t addr = libchd_cdrom_open(chd_path);
    if(addr == 1 || addr == 0){
        OutputDebugStringA("Opening CHD failed");
        return FALSE;
    }else{
        OutputDebugStringA("Opened CHD");
    }
    memcpy(&cdrf,(void*)addr,sizeof(cdrf));
    return TRUE;
}

BOOL init_chd_library(){
    libchd_module = LoadLibraryA("libchd.dll");
    if(libchd_module == NULL){
        OutputDebugStringA("Unable to load libchd.dll");
        return FALSE;
    }

    libchd_cdrom_open = (libchd_cdrom_open_fn) GetProcAddress(libchd_module, "libchd_cdrom_open");
    libchd_cdrom_get_toc = (libchd_cdrom_get_toc_fn) GetProcAddress(libchd_module,"libchd_cdrom_get_toc");
    libchd_cdrom_read_data = (libchd_cdrom_read_data_fn) GetProcAddress(libchd_module,"libchd_cdrom_read_data");

    if(libchd_cdrom_open == NULL || libchd_cdrom_read_data == NULL){
        OutputDebugStringA("libchd.dll is missing required exports");
        return FALSE;
    }

    return TRUE;
}

/*
	The Saturn Archive.org CHD Audio is Endian-Reversed for... I don't know, it is, we'll swap it while reading it out.
	Yes - I know this is a horrible way to swap this, I'll fix it later.
*/
void bswap_buffer(unsigned char* buffer,unsigned int num_bytes){
    for(unsigned int i=0;i + 1<num_bytes;i+=2){

        buffer[i] ^= buffer[i+1];
        buffer[i+1] ^= buffer[i];
        buffer[i] ^= buffer[i+1];
    }
}


void read_disc_data(unsigned char*buffer,unsigned int offset,unsigned int len,unsigned int dtype){

    const unsigned int lba_sz = 2352;
    unsigned dest_offset = 0;
    unsigned char* tb = (unsigned char*)malloc(2352);
    if(tb == NULL){
        OutputDebugStringA("Unable to allocate temporary sector buffer");
        return;
    }

    for(unsigned int i =offset;i<offset+len;i++){
        memset(tb,0x00,2352);
        int retval = libchd_cdrom_read_data(&cdrf, i,tb, dtype,1);
        if(retval == 0){
            libchd_cdrom_read_data(&cdrf,i,tb,7,1);
            bswap_buffer(tb,2352);
        }
        memcpy(buffer+dest_offset,tb,lba_sz);
        dest_offset+=2352;

    }

    free(tb);
}

typedef struct _SCSI_PASS_THROUGH_DIRECT {
    unsigned short Length;
    unsigned char  ScsiStatus;
    unsigned char  PathId;
    unsigned char  TargetId;
    unsigned char  Lun;
    unsigned char  CdbLength;
    unsigned char  SenseInfoLength;
    unsigned char  DataIn;
    unsigned int  DataTransferLength;
    unsigned int  TimeOutValue;
    void*  DataBuffer;
    unsigned int  SenseInfoOffset;
    unsigned char  Cdb[16];
}SCSI_PASS_THROUGH_DIRECT, *PSCSI_PASS_THROUGH_DIRECT;

static SCSI_PASS_THROUGH_DIRECT sptd;
unsigned char drive_id[] =
        {
                0x05, 0x80, 0x00, 0x32, 0x5B, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x43, 0x48, 0x44, 0x44, 0x72, 0x69, 0x76, 0x65,
                0x56, 0x69, 0x72, 0x74, 0x75, 0x61, 0x6C, 0x20, 0x20, 0x20, 0x20, 0x20, 0x20, 0x20, 0x20, 0x20
        };





unsigned char senseinfo[18] = {
        0x70, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x0A, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00,
        0x00, 0x00
};

BOOL __stdcall sptd_ioctl(HANDLE hDevice, DWORD dwIoControlCode, LPVOID lpInBuffer, DWORD nInBufferSize, LPVOID lpOutBuffer, DWORD nOutBufferSize, LPDWORD lpBytesReturned, LPOVERLAPPED lpOverlapped) {

    (void) hDevice;
    (void) dwIoControlCode;
    (void) nInBufferSize;
    (void) nOutBufferSize;
    (void) lpBytesReturned;
    (void) lpOverlapped;

    char info_buffer[4096] = {0x00};


    memcpy(&sptd,lpInBuffer,sizeof(sptd));
    unsigned int operation_code = sptd.Cdb[0];
    char info_msg[1024]={0x00};
    unsigned int offset;
    unsigned int lba;
    unsigned int num_blocks;
    unsigned int removal_set;
    switch(operation_code){
        case 0x00:
            OutputDebugStringA("TEST UNIT READY");
            sptd.SenseInfoLength = 0;
            memcpy(lpOutBuffer,&sptd,sptd.Length);
            return 1;
            break;

        case 0x03:
            OutputDebugStringA("REQUEST SENSE");
            sptd.Lun = 1;
            sptd.SenseInfoLength = 0;
            memcpy(sptd.DataBuffer,senseinfo,sizeof(senseinfo));
            memcpy(lpOutBuffer,&sptd,sptd.Length);
            return 1;
            break;
        case 0x12:
            OutputDebugStringA("INQUIRY");
            memcpy(sptd.DataBuffer,drive_id,32);
            sptd.Lun = 1;
            sptd.SenseInfoLength = 0;
            memcpy(lpOutBuffer,&sptd,sptd.Length);
            return 1;
            break;
        case 0x43:
            OutputDebugStringA("READ TOC/PMA/ATIP");
            if(disc_toc == NULL || disc_toc_size == 0){
                OutputDebugStringA("READ TOC requested before TOC was initialized");
                return 0;
            }
            memcpy(sptd.DataBuffer,disc_toc,disc_toc_size);
            sptd.DataTransferLength = disc_toc_size;
            sptd.SenseInfoLength = 0;
            memcpy(lpOutBuffer,&sptd,sptd.Length);
            return 1;
            break;
        case 0x1E:


            memcpy(&removal_set,sptd.Cdb+4,4);
            sprintf(info_msg,"PREVENT ALLOW MEDIUM REMOVAL set to %d",removal_set);
            OutputDebugStringA(info_msg);
            sptd.SenseInfoLength = 0;
            memcpy(lpOutBuffer,&sptd,sptd.Length);
            return 1;
            break;
        case 0xBE:


            // Copy To Data Buffer

            memcpy(&offset,sptd.Cdb+2,4);
            memcpy(&lba,sptd.Cdb+2,4);
            offset = bswap32(offset);
            lba = bswap32(lba);
            offset *= 2352;
            unsigned int num_bytes = sptd.Cdb[8];
            num_bytes *= 2352;
            sptd.SenseInfoLength = 0;
            num_blocks = sptd.Cdb[8];
            //sprintf(info_msg,"Disc Read Offset: %04X Length: %d",lba,num_blocks);
            OutputDebugStringA(info_msg);
            unsigned char*dbuf=(unsigned char*)malloc(num_blocks*2352);
            read_disc_data(dbuf,lba,num_blocks,1);

            memcpy(sptd.DataBuffer,dbuf,num_bytes);
            free(dbuf);

            memcpy(lpOutBuffer,&sptd,sptd.Length);
            return 1;
            break;
        default:
            sprintf(info_buffer,"Unknown SPTD Operation Code %#x",operation_code);
            OutputDebugStringA(info_buffer);
            return 1;
            break;
    }
}


void init_sptd(){
    OutputDebugStringA("Starting up SPTD Emulator");
    if(!init_chd_library()){
        return;
    }

    int num_args = 0;
    LPWSTR* args = CommandLineToArgvW(GetCommandLineW(),&num_args);
    if(args == NULL || num_args < 2){
        OutputDebugStringA("No CHD path was provided to SSFLoader");
        if(args != NULL){
            LocalFree(args);
        }
        return;
    }

    OutputDebugStringA("Loading CHD:");
    OutputDebugStringW(args[1]);
    if(load_chd_file(args[1])){
        get_toc_data();
    }
    LocalFree(args);
}

```

`clock-tower/README.md`:

```md
# Clock Tower script tools

Tools for the script of the Japanese Windows release of Clock Tower (Human
Entertainment): a disassembler for the scene bytecode, a text dumper and a
text injector that keeps the scene table valid after strings change length.

## Why this exists

The Windows version has better audio and an FMV intro, but it was only ever
released in Japanese. All dialogue and scene logic lives in two files,
`SCE/CT.ADO` and `SCE/CT.ADT`, in a custom bytecode. These tools are what came
out of working that format out far enough to replace the text. The full story
is in the write-up:
[Hacking Clocktower - The First Fear](https://batteryshark.github.io/2014/08/07/hacking-clocktower-the-first-fear.html).

## What is inside

| File | What it does |
|---|---|
| `adodis.py` | Disassembles `CT.ADO` into one line per instruction, split into scenes. Knows 132 opcodes, 104 of them by name. |
| `cttd.py` | Dumps every `MSGOUT` string to a tab-separated text file: offset, original byte length, text. |
| `ctti.py` | Reads the edited dump, writes a new `CT.ADO` with the new strings and a new `CT.ADT` with every scene offset shifted to match. |
| `notes.txt` | Working notes on the format from the time. |

## The format, briefly

- `CT.ADO` starts with a 256-byte header. After that it is a stream of
  instructions. Each one is a 16-bit little-endian opcode in the `0xFFxx`
  range followed by its operands.
- A scene starts with opcode `0xFF00`. `CT.ADT` is a table of 32-bit offsets,
  one per scene, pointing at those starts.
- ADT offsets are not plain file offsets. Every `0x8000` bytes of ADO data
  takes `0x10000` of ADT address space, so the upper half of each entry has to
  be halved to get the real position. Get that wrong by one byte and scenes
  stop resolving.
- Text is opcode `0xFF33` (`MSGOUT`): two 16-bit values, then a Shift-JIS
  string. The game stores a `0x00` after every single-byte character, and a
  line break is `0x0A 0x00`.
- The executable accepts a `NOREG` option that skips the registry lookup for
  the data directory and uses the game folder, which makes testing a patched
  copy easier.

## Use

Python 3, standard library only. Copy `CT.ADO` and `CT.ADT` out of the game's
`SCE` folder and keep the originals.

```sh
python3 adodis.py CT.ADO CT.ADT > script.txt     # disassemble
python3 cttd.py CT.ADO ct_txt.txt                # dump the text
# edit the third column of ct_txt.txt and save it as ct_txt_proc.txt
python3 ctti.py CT.ADO CT.ADT ct_txt_proc.txt new_CT.ADO new_CT.ADT
```

Then put the two new files back in `SCE` under their original names. Without
arguments the scripts use the file names from the original workflow
(`CT_J.ADO` and `CT_J.ADT` in, `CT.ADO` and `CT.ADT` out).

The dump is Shift-JIS, so open it in an editor that can read and save that
encoding. Leave the first two columns alone: the injector uses them to find
each string and to work out how far everything after it moves. `[NEWLINE]`
marks a line break.

## What you should see

`adodis.py` prints a listing like this:

```text
#----SCENE 0 (Offset 0x100)
RETN
JMP 23
ALLEND 19
#----SCENE 1 (Offset 0x10a)
RETN
JMP 8
```

`cttd.py` writes one line per string, 510 of them for the full script.

## Status and limits

- Checked in November 2026 on Python 3.9 against one copy of the script
  files. Dumping the text and injecting it back unchanged reproduces both
  files byte for byte. Lengthening one string moves every later scene offset
  by the right amount, and all 1,200 table entries still point at a scene
  start.
- Not re-tested in the game itself since the original work.
- Written for Python 2 in 2014 and ported to Python 3 for this repository.
- 28 opcodes are still unnamed, and several operand layouts are guesses. The
  disassembler skips ahead one byte when it meets something it cannot parse,
  so a listing can lose sync for a few instructions.
- The dumper finds strings by scanning for the `MSGOUT` opcode bytes. That
  worked for this script, but it is a scan, not a parse.
- There is no line-length check. Text that is too long for the message box
  will run off it.

No game files or translated script are included.

```

`clock-tower/adodis.py`:

```py
'''
Clocktower ADC Object File Disassembler by rFx
'''
import os,sys,binascii,struct

# Defaults are the original Japanese files, renamed. Override on the command line:
#   python3 adodis.py [CT.ADO [CT.ADT]]
ADO_FILENAME = sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else "CT_J.ADO"
ADT_FILENAME = sys.argv[2] if len(sys.argv) > 2 else "CT_J.ADT"

ADO_OP = {
0xFF00:"RETN", #Scene Prologue - 0 bytes of data. - Also an END value... the game looks to denote endings.
0xFF01:"UNK_01", # varying length data
0xFF02:"UNK_02", # 3 bytes of data
0xFF03:"UNK_03", # 3 bytes of data
0xFF04:"UNK_04", # 3 bytes of data
0xFF05:"UNK_05", # 3 bytes of data
0xFF06:"UNK_06", # 3 bytes of data
0xFF07:"UNK_07", # 3 bytes of data
0xFF0A:"UNK_0A", # 4 bytes of data. Looks like an offset to another link in the list?
0xFF0C:"UNK_0C", # 4 bytes of data
0xFF0D:"UNK_0D", # 4 bytes of data
0xFF10:"UNK_10", # 4 bytes of data
0xFF11:"UNK_11", # 4 bytes of data
0xFF12:"UNK_12", # 4 bytes of data
0xFF13:"UNK_13", # 4 bytes of data
0xFF14:"UNK_14", # 4 bytes of data
0xFF15:"UNK_15", # 4 bytes of data
0xFF16:"UNK_16", # 4 bytes of data
0xFF1F:"UNK_1F", # 0 bytes of data
0xFF20:"ALL", # 0 bytes of data. Only at the end of the ADO (twice)
#All opcodes above this are like... prologue opcodes (basically in some other list)
0xFF21:"ALLEND",  # 2 bytes of data
0xFF22:"JMP",  # 2 bytes of data - I think it uses the value for the int offset in adt as destination +adds 2
0xFF23:"CALL",  # 6 bytes of data
0xFF24:"EVDEF", # Not used in the game
0xFF25:"!!!!!!", #Not used in the game
0xFF26:"!!!!!!", #Not used in the game
0xFF27:"!!!!!!", #Not used in the game
0xFF28:"!!!!!!", #0 bytes of data.
0xFF29:"END_IF", # 4 bytes of data
0xFF2A:"WHILE",  # 4 bytes of data
0xFF2B:"NOP",    # 0 bytes of data
0xFF2C:"BREAK", # Not used in the game
0xFF2D:"ENDIF",  # 2 bytes of data
0xFF2E:"ENDWHILE",  # 2 bytes of data
0xFF2F:"ELSE",   # 2 bytes of data
0xFF30:"MSGINIT",   # 10 bytes of data
0xFF31:"MSGTYPE",   # Not used in the game
0xFF32:"MSGATTR",   # 16 bytes of data
0xFF33:"MSGOUT",   # Varying length, our in-game text uses this. :)
0xFF34:"SETMARK", #Varying length
0xFF35:"SETWAIT",  #Not used in the game
0xFF36:"MSGWAIT", #0 bytes of data
0xFF37:"EVSTART", #4 bytes of data
0xFF38:"BGFILEDISP", #Not used in the game.
0xFF39:"BGLOAD", #Varying length, normally a path to a BMP file is passed in.
0xFF3A:"PALLOAD", #Varying length. Also takes BMP files.
0xFF3B:"BGMREQ", #Varying length - loads a MIDI file into memory.
0xFF3C:"SPRCLR", #2 bytes of data.
0xFF3D:"ABSOBJANIM", #Not used in the game
0xFF3E:"OBJANIM", #Not used in the game.
0xFF3F:"ALLSPRCLR", #0 bytes of data
0xFF40:"MSGCLR", #0 bytes 0f data
0xFF41:"SCREENCLR", #0 bytes of data
0xFF42:"SCREENON", #0 bytes of data
0xFF43:"SCREENOFF", #0 bytes of data
0xFF44:"SCREENIN", # Not used in the game.
0xFF45:"SCREENOUT", # Not used in the game.
0xFF46:"BGDISP", # Always 12 bytes of data.
0xFF47:"BGANIM", #14 bytes of data.
0xFF48:"BGSCROLL",#10 bytes of data.
0xFF49:"PALSET", #10 bytes of data.
0xFF4A:"BGWAIT", #0 bytes of data.
0xFF4B:"WAIT", #4 bytes of data.
0xFF4C:"BWAIT", #Not used in the game.
0xFF4D:"BOXFILL", #14 bytes of data.
0xFF4E:"BGCLR", # Not used in the game.
0xFF4F:"SETBKCOL", #6 bytes of data.
0xFF50:"MSGCOL", #Not used in the game.
0xFF51:"MSGSPD", #2 bytes of data.
0xFF52:"MAPINIT", #12 bytes of data.
0xFF53:"MAPLOAD", #Two Paths... Sometimes NULL NULL - Loads the background blit bmp and the map file to load it.
0xFF54:"MAPDISP", #Not used in the game.
0xFF55:"SPRENT", #16 bytes of data.
0xFF56:"SETPROC", #2 bytes of data.
0xFF57:"SCEINIT", #0 bytes of data.
0xFF58:"USERCTL", #2 bytes of data.
0xFF59:"MAPATTR", #2 bytes of data.
0xFF5A:"MAPPOS", #6 bytes of data.
0xFF5B:"SPRPOS", #8 bytes of data.
0xFF5C:"SPRANIM", #8 bytes of data.
0xFF5D:"SPRDIR", #10 bytes of data.
0xFF5E:"GAMEINIT", #0 bytes of data.
0xFF5F:"CONTINIT", #0 bytes of data.
0xFF60:"SCEEND", #0 bytes of data.
0xFF61:"MAPSCROLL", #6 bytes of data.
0xFF62:"SPRLMT", #6 bytes of data.
0xFF63:"SPRWALKX", #10 bytes of data.
0xFF64:"ALLSPRDISP", #Not used in the game.
0xFF65:"MAPWRT", #Not used in the game.
0xFF66:"SPRWAIT", #2 bytes of data.
0xFF67:"SEREQ", #Varying length - loads a .WAV file.
0xFF68:"SNDSTOP", #0 bytes of data.
0xFF69:"SESTOP", #Varying length - specifies a .WAV to stop or ALL for all sounds.
0xFF6A:"BGMSTOP", #0 bytes of data.
0xFF6B:"DOORNOSET", #0 bytes of data.
0xFF6C:"RAND", #6 bytes of data.
0xFF6D:"BTWAIT", #2 bytes of data
0xFF6E:"FAWAIT", #0 bytes of data
0xFF6F:"SCLBLOCK", #Varying length - no idea.
0xFF70:"EVSTOP", #Not used in the game.
0xFF71:"SEREQPV", #Varying length - .WAV path input, I think this is to play and repeat.
0xFF72:"SEREQSPR", #Varying length - .WAV path input, I think this is like SEREQPV except different somehow.
0xFF73:"SCERESET", #0 bytes of data.
0xFF74:"BGSPRENT", #12 bytes of data.
0xFF75:"BGSPRPOS", #Not used in the game.
0xFF76:"BGSPRSET", #Not used in the game.
0xFF77:"SLANTSET", #8 bytes of data.
0xFF78:"SLANTCLR", #0 bytes of data.
0xFF79:"DUMMY", #Not used in the game.
0xFF7A:"SPCFUNC", #Varying length - usage uncertain.
0xFF7B:"SEPAN", #Varying length - guessing to set the L/R of Stereo SE.
0xFF7C:"SEVOL", #Varying length - guessing toe set the volume level of SE 
0xFF7D:"BGDISPTRN", #14 bytes of data.
0xFF7E:"DEBUG", #Not used in the game.
0xFF7F:"TRACE", #Not used in the game.
0xFF80:"TMWAIT", #4 bytes of data.
0xFF81:"BGSPRANIM", #18 bytes of data.
0xFF82:"ABSSPRENT", #Not used in the game.
0xFF83:"NEXTCOM", #2 bytes of data.
0xFF84:"WORKCLR", #0 bytes of data.
0xFF85:"BGBUFCLR", #4 bytes of data.
0xFF86:"ABSBGSPRENT", #12 bytes of data.
0xFF87:"AVIPLAY", #This one is used only once - to load the intro AVI file.
0xFF88:"AVISTOP", #0 bytes of data.
0xFF89:"SPRMARK", #Only used in PSX Version.
0xFF8A:"BGMATTR",#Only used in PSX Version.
#BIG GAP IN OPCODES... maybe not even in existence.
0xFFA0:"UNK_A0", #12 bytes of data.
0xFFB0:"UNK_B0", #12 bytes of data.
0xFFDF:"UNK_DF", #2 bytes of data.
0xFFE0:"UNK_E0", #0 bytes of data.
0xFFEA:"UNK_EA", #0 bytes of data.
0xFFEF:"UNK_EF" #12 bytes of data.
}

if(__name__=="__main__"):
	print("#Disassembling ADO/ADT...")
	#Read ADO/ADT Data to memory.
	f = open(ADO_FILENAME,"rb")
	ado_data = f.read()
	f.close()
	f = open(ADT_FILENAME,"rb")
	adt_data = f.read()
	f.close()
	scene_count = -1
	#Skip ADO Header
	i = 256
	while i < (len(ado_data) -1):
		cur_val = struct.unpack("<H",ado_data[i:i+2])[0]

		if(cur_val in ADO_OP.keys()):
			#0xFF00
			if(cur_val == 0xFF00):
				scene_count +=1
				print("#----SCENE %d (Offset %#x)" % (scene_count,i)) 
				print(ADO_OP[cur_val])
				i+=2

 or cur_val == 0xFF88 or cur_val == 0xFF78 or cur_val == 0xFF73 or cur_val == 0xFF6E or cur_val == 0xFF6B

				print("%s %#x %#x %#x %#x %#x" % (ADO_OP[cur_val],val_1,val_2,val_3,val_4,val_5))

				i+=2

				endstr_offset = ado_data[i:].index(b"\xff")

				print("%s %#x %#x %#x %#x %#x %#x %#x %#x" % (ADO_OP[cur_val],val_1,val_2,val_3,val_4,val_5,val_6,val_7,val_8))
			elif(cur_val == 0xFF34):
				i+=2

				i+= len(instr)

				i+=2
			elif(cur_val in range(0xFF39,0xFF3C) or cur_val == 0xFF67):
				i+=2 
				val_1 = struct.unpack("<H",ado_data[i:i+2])[0]
				i+=2
				endstr_offset = ado_data[i:].index(b"\xff") - 1
				instr = ado_data[i:i+endstr_offset]
				i+= len(instr)
				if(instr.find(b"\x00\x00\x00") != -1):
					finstr = instr[:instr.index(b"\x00")].decode("ascii","replace")
					val_2 = struct.unpack("b",instr[instr.index(b"\x00")+1:instr.index(b"\x00")+2])[0]
					val_3 = struct.unpack("b",instr[instr.index(b"\x00")+2:])[0]
					print("%s %#x %s %#x %#x" % (ADO_OP[cur_val],val_1,finstr,val_2,val_3))
				elif(instr.find(b"\x00\x00") != -1):
					finstr = instr[:instr.index(b"\x00")].decode("ascii","replace")
					val_2 = struct.unpack("b",instr[instr.index(b"\x00")+1:])[0]
					print("%s %#x %s %#x" % (ADO_OP[cur_val],val_1,finstr,val_2))

`clock-tower/cttd.py`:

```py
'''
 CTD - Clocktower Text Dumper by rFx

 Usage: python3 cttd.py [CT_J.ADO [ct_txt.txt]]

 Writes one line per MSGOUT string: offset, original byte length, text.
 The text column is Shift-JIS, exactly as stored in the game minus padding.
'''

import os,sys,struct,binascii

ADO_FILENAME = sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else "CT_J.ADO"
TXT_FILENAME = sys.argv[2] if len(sys.argv) > 2 else "ct_txt.txt"

f = open(ADO_FILENAME,"rb")
data = f.read()
f.close()
g = open(TXT_FILENAME,"wb")
for i in range(0,len(data)-1):
	if(data[i:i+2] == b'\x33\xff'):
		#We have to skip 6 because of the opcodes and values we don't care about changing.
		i+=6
		str_offset = i
		str_end = data[i:].index(b'\xff') -1
		newstr = data[i:i+str_end]
		strlen = len(newstr)
		newstr = newstr.replace(b"\x0a\x00",b"[NEWLINE]")
		#The game puts nulls after any ASCII character, we need to remove those.
		newstr = newstr.replace(b"\x00",b"")
		g.write(("%#x\t%d\t" % (str_offset,strlen)).encode("ascii"))
		g.write(newstr)
		g.write(b"\n")

g.close()

```

`clock-tower/ctti.py`:

```py
'''
Clocktower Text Injector by rFx

Usage: python3 ctti.py [CT_J.ADO [CT_J.ADT [ct_txt_proc.txt [CT.ADO [CT.ADT]]]]]

Reads the original script pair and an edited text dump (same layout as the
one cttd.py writes), then writes a new CT.ADO with the replaced strings and
a new CT.ADT with every scene offset moved by the bytes gained or lost
before it.
'''
import os,sys,struct,binascii

def arg(n,default):
	return sys.argv[n] if len(sys.argv) > n else default

ADO_IN = arg(1,"CT_J.ADO")
ADT_IN = arg(2,"CT_J.ADT")
TXT_IN = arg(3,"ct_txt_proc.txt")
ADO_OUT = arg(4,"CT.ADO")
ADT_OUT = arg(5,"CT.ADT")

def is_ascii(s):
    return all(ord(c) < 128 for c in s)

# ADT entries are not plain file offsets: every 0x8000 bytes of ADO data
# takes up 0x10000 of ADT address space.
def get_real_offset(offset):
	#Get High Value
	high_val = offset & 0xFFFF0000
	high_val //= 2
	low_val = offset & 0xFFFF
	return high_val+low_val
def get_fake_offset(offset):
	#Get High Value
	mult = int(offset / 0x8000)
	shft_val = 0x8000 * mult

	low_val = offset & 0xFFFF

	return offset + shft_val


f = open(ADO_IN,"rb")
data = f.read()
f.close()

offset_vals = []
f = open(TXT_IN,"rb")
lines = f.readlines()
f.close()
o,l,s = lines[0].split(b"\t",2)
first_offset = int(o,16)
newdata = data[:first_offset]

for i in range(0,len(lines)):
	line = lines[i]
	offset, osl, instr = line.split(b"\t",2)
	offset = int(offset,16)

	instr = instr.rstrip(b'\n')

	instr = instr.replace(b"[NEWLINE]",b"\x0a")
	#Fix the ASCII characters.
	instr = instr.decode("SHIFT-JIS")
	newstr = ""
	for char in instr:
		if(is_ascii(char)):
			newstr+=char+'\x00'
		else:
			newstr+=char
	instr = newstr
	instr = instr.encode("SHIFT-JIS")
	newstrlen = len(instr)
	osl = int(osl)
	strldiff = newstrlen - osl

	#Replace the data
	if(i < len(lines)-1):
		nextline = lines[i+1]
		nextoffset,nsl,nstr = nextline.split(b"\t",2)
		offset_vals.append({"offset":offset,"val":strldiff})
		nextoffset = int(nextoffset,16)

		newdata += instr+data[offset+osl:nextoffset]
	else:
		#End of last string to EOF
		offset_vals.append({"offset":offset,"val":strldiff})
		newdata += instr + data[offset+osl:]

#Write new ADO File.
g = open(ADO_OUT,"wb")
g.write(newdata)
g.close()

#Fix up the ADT file.
f = open(ADT_IN,"rb")
datat = f.read()
f.close()
g = open(ADT_OUT,"wb")

for i in range(0,len(datat),4):
	cur_offset = get_real_offset(struct.unpack("<I",datat[i:i+4])[0])
	final_adj = 0
	for offset in offset_vals:

		if(cur_offset > offset["offset"]):
			final_adj += offset["val"]
	g.write(struct.pack("<I",get_fake_offset(cur_offset + final_adj)))
g.close()

```

`clock-tower/notes.txt`:

```txt
Clocktower's text and various other logic operates off of two files:
CT.ADO and CT.ADT - their relation is not yet discovered

CT.ADO contains text strings in SHIFT-JIS format, precluded by two big endian short values and another little endian opcode value... the opcode tells the game what kind of text it is and what it should do with it.

Printable strings appear to have the same starting pattern: "0x33ff00f000f0" and end with 0x0A 0x00 0x0A 0x00 or just 0x00

The game has a NOREG option discovered near the registry query code. It skips the data-directory registry check and assumes the data lives in the game root, which is useful for this patching workflow.

String Prologue:
"\xff\x33\x00\xf0\x00\xf0"

STRING CONTENT
(Normally ends with 0x00 except when leaving space with 0x00 0x0A 0x00) Look for opcodes... thats the only way to tell
Next Opcodes:

BIG_ENDIAN opcodes / LITTLE_ENDIAN
0xFF00 -- CT_UNK // Normally the prologue of a scene
0xFF01 -- CT_UNK // first one had 3 bytes of 0x00 0x01 0x00 , second was three nulls
0xFF08 -- CT_UNK // has a null at the end
0xFF09 -- CT_UNK // has a null at the end
0xFF11 -- CT_UNK // had an int (first one being 0x1C019) (maybe 2 shorts) second was 0xC019 and 0x00
0xFF17 -- CT_UNK
0xFF1F -- CT_UNK
0xFF21 -- CT_UNK // 2 byte value at the end -- 0x13 for the first one, 0x05 for the second
0xFF22 -- CT_UNK // 2 byte value at the end -- 0x59 for the first one 
0xFF28 -- CT_UNK
0xFF29 -- CT_UNK // 2x2 byte value at the end for second (0x1 and 0x1)
0xFF2B -- CT_UNK
0xFF2D -- CT_UNK // 2 byte value at the end (first was 0x01)
0xFF30 -- CT_UNK
0xFF33 -- CT_STRING
0xFF36 -- CT_UNK
0xFF39 -- CT_FILEPATH // (3 bytes - 0xff 0x00 0x00) with an ASCII path(.bmp)then two nulls
0xFF3B -- CT_FILEPATH // a 16-bit int (0x1) followed by an ASCII path (first one is a .MID file) and 3 bytes of null
0xFF40 -- CT_UNK
0xFF46 -- CT_UNK // has 12 bytes following -- first two shorts are 0x20 and 0x148
0xFF4B -- CT_UNK
0xFF6A -- CT_UNK


Each SCENE_SEGMENT starts with an 0x00 that CT.ADT points to. There appear to be offset aliases or cross-references in the data, so being off by even one byte can break scene resolution. Scenes may cross-reference each other by hardcoded offsets in ADO; however, adding values to a scene without subtracting 256 and removing the 256-byte ADO header still worked in one test.

```

`culdcept/README.md`:

```md
# Culdcept data tools

Scripts and format notes from reverse engineering the data files of Culdcept
on the Dreamcast and PlayStation 2: the nested archives, the card records, the
sprites and the board maps.

## Why this exists

The game keeps its cards, sprites and maps in one large file with no names,
no directory and headerless compressed streams. Getting anything out meant
working out the container, then the compression, then each record type. The
write-up covers the container and the compression:
[Culdcept Series](https://batteryshark.github.io/2013/11/12/culdcept-series.html).

## What is inside

| File | What it does | State |
|---|---|---|
| `culdroot.py` | Splits a container whose table is (offset, size) pairs. Pass `b` as a second argument for big-endian tables. | Python 3, tested |
| `culddat.py` | Splits a container whose table is offsets only. | Python 3, tested |
| `card_decrypt.py` | Decodes the hidden cards. The comment at the top walks through how the cipher was worked out. | Python 3, tested |
| `card_parser.py` | Reads a card record and writes its stats as JSON, the card art and the sprite as PNG. | Python 2.7, not run |
| `sprcvrt.py` | Converts a sprite (16-colour or 256-colour palette, ARGB1555) to PNG. | Python 2.7, not run |
| `mapdump.py` | Decompresses a board map's 64x64 tiles and composites them into one PNG. | Python 2.7, not run |
| `card-format-notes.txt` | Field-by-field layout of a card record. | |
| `animation-format-notes.txt` | Partial notes on the sprite animation files. | |

## What was found

- **Containers nest.** The top-level file is a table of offsets and sizes
  followed by data, and many of the entries are containers again.
- **Compression is LH5 without the header.** Compressed entries start with
  `0x08` and go straight into the Huffman stream, so a normal LHA tool will
  not touch them until a header is put back.
- **Some cards are scrambled on top of that.** They are flagged with `0x09`
  instead of `0x08`. After decompression every 16-bit word is stored as the
  difference from the one before it, so adding each word to its predecessor
  recovers the record. The known stats of one hidden card were enough to spot
  the pattern.
- **Images are raw ARGB1555.** Card art is 256x320. Board maps are grids of
  separately compressed 64x64 tiles behind a table of offsets, with one extra
  padding column the game does not draw.

## Use

The three tested scripts need only Python 3:

```sh
python3 culdroot.py DATA.BIN          # -> DATA.part1.bin, DATA.part2.bin, ...
python3 culddat.py DATA.part7.bin     # -> DATA.part7.part00001.bin, ...
python3 card_decrypt.py card.bin      # -> card.dec
```

The other three are Python 2.7 and need `Pillow`. `card_parser.py` and
`mapdump.py` also call an external LH5 decompressor named `delha`, which reads
`in.tmp` and writes `out.tmp`. That program is not in this repository, so those
two scripts document the formats more than they run out of the box.

## Status and limits

- `culdroot.py`, `culddat.py` and `card_decrypt.py` were checked in November
  2026 on Python 3.9 with files built to the layouts above, not with game
  data.
- `card_parser.py`, `sprcvrt.py` and `mapdump.py` are as originally written and
  were not run: no Python 2, no `Pillow` and no `delha` on the machine used.
- Many card fields are still unknown. They are labelled `unk_` in the parser
  and `???` in the notes.
- The tools were written against the Dreamcast and PS2 releases. Nothing here
  handles the DS or later games.

No game files, card art or maps are included.

```

`culdcept/animation-format-notes.txt`:

```txt
Culdcept Animation Format ("those .bin files")

0x00-0x02 short byte length of file

Statue is 32 big (8*4)

{ #Seems to be the upper right src point
0 - 0x18 24
2 - 0x01
}  

4  - 0x02 
6  - 0x2D 45 #Width - dest
8  - 0x37 55 #height - src
10 - 0xFFEE -22
12 - 0xFFCF -49 # Height - dest start
14 - 0x00
16 - 0x00
18 - -1
20 - -1
22 - 0xFFEC  -20
24 - 0x10 16
26 - 0x00 #Width Dest offset (nothing because its fine)
28 - 0xFFFC -4 #Height Dest offset (-49+ -4 = -55)

```

Notes
99 - kobold

Card Format

	0x02 Armor

	0x0B Weapons and Armor and Scrolls
